{"alias_target":null,"confidence":"high","current_chinese":"\"我可以蓬起羽毛，看起来真凶\n 当那个老家伙，这里我说了算\n 我是只昂首阔步、梳理羽毛的矮脚公鸡，在找架打。\"\n    -Andrew Bird's Bowl of Fire，《Cock o' the Walk》。1998年。","current_english":"“I can puff up my feathers, look real mean\n Be the old man that this here's scene\n I'm a struttin' preenin' bantam rooster looking for a fight.”\n    -Andrew Bird's Bowl of Fire, “Cock o' the Walk”. 1998.","decision_authority":"Issue #72 逐项人工审阅；术语以当前 docs/glossary.md 与 ZH source.txt 为准。","deferral_owner":null,"deferral_reason":null,"dependency_group":"Non-unique monsters","display_context":"QuotesDB 直接引文；由帮助/描述界面的物品、法术、能力、怪物或地形详情按英文 canonical key 查询并显示。","evidence_locations":["crawl-ref/source/dat/descript/quotes.txt:4093","crawl-ref/source/dat/descript/zh/quotes.txt:3478","crawl-ref/source/database.cc@getQuoteString","docs/glossary.md@366e807eaae5403b6c3925df5970cd237b447ead76fdb717b71273473b5db67e"],"fact_sha256":"0b22ee2c401e1fe02fc8a65fac2806f0108f4290f9cd956fb024e88c22653a25","glossary_authority":"docs/glossary.md SHA-256 366e807eaae5403b6c3925df5970cd237b447ead76fdb717b71273473b5db67e","identity":"quotes:tengu warrior","key":"tengu warrior","lifecycle":"direct-quote","literary_exception_reason":null,"producer_consumer":"TextDB(\"quotes\", \"descript/\") → locale overlay descript/zh/quotes.txt → getQuoteString(lowercase key, localized lookup with EN fallback, alias resolution) → quote pane consumers","proposed_translation":null,"reentry_trigger":"当该 EN/ZH block、canonical key、别名目标、QuotesDB 加载/查询消费者、Issue #3 专名裁定或 docs/glossary.md 权威变化时重审。","rejected_alternatives":["仅因偏好不同改换文学译本、书名或标点；缺乏可证明的语义收益。"],"resolved_key":"tengu warrior","reviewer_rationale":"逐句核对完整英文、当前中文、署名与上下文后，核心命题、专名指代和语气均无可证明偏差；保持现译。","section":"Non-unique monsters","suggestions":[],"terminal_conclusion":"keep"}
{"alias_target":null,"confidence":"high","current_chinese":"\"从那巨怪地窖中那个豁开的陷阱门里渗出翻涌而出的，是\n 一尊如此难以置信的巨兽般的怪物，我无法怀疑其原型仅凭\n 目视便足以杀人的力量。即使现在，我仍无法用我所能驾驭\n 的任何词语来形容它。我可以称它为巨大的——长触须的——\n 长鼻的——章鱼眼的——半无定形的——可塑的——部分有鳞、\n 部分有皱的——呃！\"\n    -H.P.洛夫克拉夫特与黑兹尔·希尔德，《自万古而出》，\n     《诡丽幻谭》，第25卷第4期，第478-96页。1935年4月。","current_english":"“Oozing and surging up out of that yawning trap-door in the Cyclopean crypt I\n had glimpsed such an unbelievable behemothic monstrosity that I could not\n doubt the power of its original to kill with its mere sight. Even now I cannot\n begin to suggest it with any words at my command. I might call it gigantic —\n tentacled — proboscidian — octopus-eyed — semi-amorphous — plastic — partly\n squamous and partly rugose — ugh!”\n    -H.P. Lovecraft and Hazel Heald, “Out of the Aeons”, _Weird Tales_, 25, No.\n     4, pp. 478-96. April 1935.","decision_authority":"Issue #72 逐项人工审阅；术语以当前 docs/glossary.md 与 ZH source.txt 为准。","deferral_owner":null,"deferral_reason":null,"dependency_group":"Non-unique monsters","display_context":"QuotesDB 直接引文；由帮助/描述界面的物品、法术、能力、怪物或地形详情按英文 canonical key 查询并显示。","evidence_locations":["crawl-ref/source/dat/descript/quotes.txt:4100","crawl-ref/source/dat/descript/zh/quotes.txt:3485","crawl-ref/source/database.cc@getQuoteString","docs/glossary.md@366e807eaae5403b6c3925df5970cd237b447ead76fdb717b71273473b5db67e"],"fact_sha256":"2ea976faf33acfbd0dc74d34bea2e7a9f56b3be1528752d6903b9159c6c02546","glossary_authority":"docs/glossary.md SHA-256 366e807eaae5403b6c3925df5970cd237b447ead76fdb717b71273473b5db67e","identity":"quotes:tentacled monstrosity","key":"tentacled monstrosity","lifecycle":"direct-quote","literary_exception_reason":null,"producer_consumer":"TextDB(\"quotes\", \"descript/\") → locale overlay descript/zh/quotes.txt → getQuoteString(lowercase key, localized lookup with EN fallback, alias resolution) → quote pane consumers","proposed_translation":null,"reentry_trigger":"当该 EN/ZH block、canonical key、别名目标、QuotesDB 加载/查询消费者、Issue #3 专名裁定或 docs/glossary.md 权威变化时重审。","rejected_alternatives":["仅因偏好不同改换文学译本、书名或标点；缺乏可证明的语义收益。"],"resolved_key":"tentacled monstrosity","reviewer_rationale":"逐句核对完整英文、当前中文、署名与上下文后，核心命题、专名指代和语气均无可证明偏差；保持现译。","section":"Non-unique monsters","suggestions":[],"terminal_conclusion":"keep"}
{"alias_target":null,"confidence":"high","current_chinese":"\"另一边，泰坦们也急切地加强阵势，双方同时展示着他们\n 双手的力量和威力。无边的海洋可怖地咆哮，大地轰然作响：\n 辽阔的天空颤抖呻吟，高耸的奥林匹斯山在永生众神的冲击\n 下从根基动摇，沉重的震撼直达幽暗的塔耳塔罗斯，还有\n 他们在那可怕的突袭和猛烈投石中沉重的脚步声。于是，\n 他们彼此投掷着致命的飞弹，两军呐喊的呼声直达星空；\n 他们以惊天动地的战吼交汇在一起。\"\n    -赫西俄德，《神谱》，公元前8世纪。\n     H.G.伊夫林-怀特英译，1914年。","current_english":"“And on the other part the Titans eagerly strengthened their ranks, and both\n sides at one time showed the work of their hands and their might. The\n boundless sea rang terribly around, and the earth crashed loudly: wide Heaven\n was shaken and groaned, and high Olympus reeled from its foundation under the\n charge of the undying gods, and a heavy quaking reached dim Tartarus and the\n deep sound of their feet in the fearful onset and of their hard missiles. So,\n then, they launched their grievous shafts upon one another, and the cry of\n both armies as they shouted reached to starry heaven; and they met together\n with a great battle-cry.”\n    -Hesiod, _Theogony_, 8th cent. B.C.\n     trans. H.G. {"alias_target":null,"confidence":"high","current_chinese":"\"白天温柔的袜子宠爱它们，皮革缝制的鞋子加固它们，\n 但我的脚趾几乎浑然不觉。它们唯一感兴趣的，就是长出\n 脚趾甲——那种半透明、柔韧的角质薄片，作为一种防御\n ——防御谁呢？\"\n    -豪尔赫·路易斯·博尔赫斯，《脚趾甲》。1960年。\n     安德鲁·赫尔利英译，1998年。","current_english":"“Gentle socks pamper them by day, and shoes cobbled of leather fortify them,\n but my toes hardly notice. {"alias_target":null,"confidence":"high","current_chinese":"\"你之于我，是一种甜蜜的折磨。\"\n    -拉尔夫·沃尔多·爱默生，《随笔：第一辑》，第六篇：友谊。1841年。","current_english":"“Thou art to me a delicious torment.”\n    -Ralph Waldo Emerson, _Essays: First Series_, Essay VI: Friendship. 1841.","decision_authority":"Issue #72 逐项人工审阅；术语以当前 docs/glossary.md 与 ZH source.txt 为准。","deferral_owner":null,"deferral_reason":null,"dependency_group":"Non-unique monsters","display_context":"QuotesDB 直接引文；由帮助/描述界面的物品、法术、能力、怪物或地形详情按英文 canonical key 查询并显示。","evidence_locations":["crawl-ref/source/dat/descript/quotes.txt:4140","crawl-ref/source/dat/descript/zh/quotes.txt:3523","crawl-ref/source/database.cc@getQuoteString","docs/glossary.md@366e807eaae5403b6c3925df5970cd237b447ead76fdb717b71273473b5db67e"],"fact_sha256":"1c62a1cef0262413466daa441e76f12bfd0c7a521c37829b7c4e44e31dc05de1","glossary_authority":"docs/glossary.md SHA-256 366e807eaae5403b6c3925df5970cd237b447ead76fdb717b71273473b5db67e","identity":"quotes:tormentor","key":"tormentor","lifecycle":"direct-quote","literary_exception_reason":null,"producer_consumer":"TextDB(\"quotes\", \"descript/\") → locale overlay descript/zh/quotes.txt → getQuoteString(lowercase key, localized lookup with EN fallback, alias resolution) → quote pane consumers","proposed_translation":null,"reentry_trigger":"当该 EN/ZH block、canonical key、别名目标、QuotesDB 加载/查询消费者、Issue #3 专名裁定或 docs/glossary.md 权威变化时重审。","rejected_alternatives":["仅因偏好不同改换文学译本、书名或标点；缺乏可证明的语义收益。"],"resolved_key":"tormentor","reviewer_rationale":"逐句核对完整英文、当前中文、署名与上下文后，核心命题、专名指代和语气均无可证明偏差；保持现译。","section":"Non-unique monsters","suggestions":[],"terminal_conclusion":"keep"}
{"alias_target":null,"confidence":"high","current_chinese":"\"蜗牛，蜗牛，慢如鼻涕虫，\n 把你的四只角给我看；\n 你若是不给我看四只角，\n 我就把你扔出门外，\n 让沟里的乌鸦\n 拿你当面包和黄油吃。\"\n    -西里西亚童谣。S.W.辛格引用于《笔记与查询》第69期。1851年。","current_english":"“Snail, snail, slug-slow,\n To me thy four horns show;\n If thou dost not show me thy four,\n I will throw thee out of the door,\n For the crow in the gutter,\n To eat for bread and butter.”\n    -Silesian rhyme. {"alias_target":null,"confidence":"high","current_chinese":"\"驱使沙伯替为亡者在冥界劳作的咒语：\n 哦，分派给我的沙伯替，若我被召唤或指派去做冥界中\n 必须完成的任何工作，若确实有任何障碍因此被植入给你\n 如同一个尽责之人，你当在每次开垦田地、修筑堤岸或\n 将沙土自东运至西的事上为我效力；'我在此'，你当说。\"\n    -第6号咒语，《亡灵书》，引自《古埃及亡灵书：穿越来世\n     之旅》，约翰·H·泰勒编。2010年。","current_english":"“Spell for causing a shabti to do work for a man in the realm of the dead:\n O shabti, allotted to me, if I be summoned or if I be detailed to do any work\n which has to be done in the realm of the dead, if indeed any obstacles are\n implanted for you therewith as a man at his duties, you shall detail yourself\n for me on every occasion of making arable the fields, of flooding the banks or\n of conveying sand from east to west; ‘Here I am’, you shall say.\n\n    -Spell 6, _Book of the Dead_, from _Ancient Egyptian Book of the Dead:\n     Journey through the afterlife_, edited by John H. {"alias_target":null,"confidence":"high","current_chinese":"\"那怪异的植物蓓蕾……以超乎寻常的速度再次从法尔默的\n 头上长了出来。一根可憎的淡绿色茎干正粗壮地向上生长，\n 长到六七英寸高时开始像鹿角一样分叉。\n\n 比这更可怕的——如果还有可能的话——是从眼睛里长出了\n 类似的生长物；它们的茎干垂直攀过前额，完全取代了\n 眼球。\"\n    -克拉克·阿什顿·史密斯，《坟墓之种》。1933年。","current_english":"“The monstrous plant bud . . . had grown again with preternatural rapidity,\n from Falmer's head. {"alias_target":null,"confidence":"high","current_chinese":"\"无数恶魔与龙的雕塑呈现出阴郁的面貌。火焰躁动的光芒\n 使它们仿佛在眼前移动。有些狮鹫似乎在笑，有些滴水兽\n 仿佛在嚎叫，有些沙罗曼达朝着火焰喷气，有些塔拉斯各\n 在烟雾中打喷嚏。在这些被火焰和喧嚣从石头的沉睡中唤醒\n 的怪物中，有一个在走动，不时看见它穿过燃烧柴堆那发光\n 的表面，如同一只蝙蝠掠过烛光。\"\n    -维克多·雨果，《巴黎圣母院》，第十卷第四章。1831年。\n     伊莎贝尔·F·哈普古德英译。","current_english":"“Their innumerable sculptures of demons and dragons assumed a lugubrious\n aspect. The restless light of the flame made them move to the eye. There were\n griffins which had the air of laughing, gargoyles which one fancied one heard\n yelping, salamanders which puffed at the fire, tarasques which sneezed in the\n smoke. And among the monsters thus roused from their sleep of stone by this\n flame, by this noise, there was one who walked about, and who was seen, from\n time to time, to pass across the glowing face of the pile, like a bat in front\n of a candle.”\n    -Victor Hugo, _The Hunchback of Notre-Dame_, 10, ch. IV. 1831.\n     trans. Isabel F. {"alias_target":null,"confidence":"high","current_chinese":"\"我对19世纪末那个问题'电动力学想告诉我们什么'的完整\n 回答，简单来说就是：\n\n 虚空中的场具有物理实在性；支撑它们的介质则没有。\n\n 在消除了电动力学的神秘之后，请允许我立即对量子力学\n 做同样的事：\n\n 关联具有物理实在性；被关联的事物则没有。\"\n    -N.大卫·默明，《量子力学想告诉我们什么？》，1998年。","current_english":"“My complete answer to the late 19th century question 'what is electrodynamics\n trying to tell us' would simply be this:\n\n Fields in empty space have physical reality; the medium that supports them does\n not.\n\n Having thus removed the mystery from electrodynamics, let me immediately do the\n same for quantum mechanics:\n\n Correlations have physical reality; that which they correlate does not.”\n    -N. {"alias_target":null,"confidence":"high","current_chinese":"\"他的眼睛紧盯着那扇门；门缓缓开启，显现出一个身形\n 威严却面容阴沉的陌生人，严厉地质问他为何被召唤。'我\n 没有召唤你，'那颤抖的学生说道。'你召唤了！'陌生人\n 愤怒地逼近，说道；'恶魔可不是白白就能召唤的。'学生\n 无言以对；那恶魔因一个未经入门者竟敢仅凭妄念便召唤他\n 而大怒，抓住学生的喉咙，将他勒死了。\n\n 几天后，阿格里帕回来时，发现他的房子被群魔包围了。\n 有些恶魔坐在烟囱顶上，在空中踢着腿；还有些在女儿墙\n 边缘玩着跳背游戏。他的书房里挤满了恶魔，他几乎无法\n 走到书桌前。\"\n    -查尔斯·麦凯，《异常流行幻象与群体疯狂回忆录》，\n     第三卷，第一部分。1841年。","current_english":"“He fixed his eyes upon the door, which, slowly opening, disclosed a stranger\n of majestic form, but scowling features, who demanded sternly, why he was\n summoned? ‘I did not summon you,’ said the trembling student. ‘You did!’ said\n the stranger, advancing, angrily; ‘and the demons are not to be invoked in\n vain.’ The student could make no reply; and the demon, enraged that one of the\n uninitiated should have summoned him out of mere presumption, seized him by\n the throat and strangled him.\n\n When Agrippa returned, a few days afterwards, he found his house beset with\n devils. Some of them were sitting on the chimneypots, kicking up their legs in\n the air; while others were playing at leapfrog, on the very edge of the\n parapet. {"alias_target":null,"confidence":"high","current_chinese":"\"看起来，丧尸虽然从坟墓中出来，却既不是鬼魂，也不是\n 像拉撒路那样从死里复活的人。据说，丧尸是一具没有灵魂\n 的人类尸体，仍是死的，只是被人从坟墓中挖出，通过巫术\n 赋予了一种机械的生命表象——它是一具被驱使着像活人一样\n 行走、行动和移动的死尸。拥有这种能力的人会去一座新坟，\n 在尸体还未腐烂之前将其挖出，用电流刺激其活动，然后将\n 它变成仆人或奴隶，偶尔用来犯下某些罪行，更多的时候\n 只是作为住所或农场周围的苦力，给它安排沉重乏味的劳作，\n 如果它懈怠，就像对待哑巴牲畜一样鞭打它。\"\n    -威廉·西布鲁克，《魔幻岛》。1929年。\n\n\"它们不怕！无论我们做什么，无论我们杀多少，它们永远、\n 永远不会害怕！\"\n    -马克斯·布鲁克斯，《僵尸世界大战：一部僵尸战争口述史》。2006年。","current_english":"“It seemed that while the zombie came from the grave, it was neither a ghost,\n nor yet a person who had been raised like Lazarus from the dead. The zombie,\n they say, is a soulless human corpse, still dead, but taken from the grave and\n endowed by sorcery with a mechanical semblance of life—it is a dead body which\n is made to walk and act and move as if it were alive. People who have the\n power to do this go to a fresh grave, dig up the body before it has had time\n to rot, galvanize it into movement, and then make of it a servant or slave,\n occasionally for the commission of some crime, more often simply as a drudge\n around the habitation or the farm, setting it dull heavy tasks, and beating it\n like a dumb beast if it slackens.”\n    -William Seabrook, _The Magic Island_. 1929.\n\n“They’re not afraid!
